## [2026-09-04] note | CS-11 — the multirate defect gets a function, and a gradient gets a horizon

**Dating.** Measured 2026-09-03, written 2026-09-04, and both are on the record: the run finished at 22:01 and this page did not. Tier 15's rule is to verify the environment clock before stamping, and one date here would have been wrong by the other.

**Why.** [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4's Phase B third row and §6's last open critical-path blocker. **W90** has stood open since Tier 17 with the sharpest statement on the worklist: *R9 covers the flux transient and is $62\times$ too small; the lag over a long exchange interval is $\sigma$, and nothing bounds it as a function of that interval.* R4 forbids shrinking the exchange interval below $\max_i \Delta t_i$, so the obvious remedy is the one axis a frozen expert cannot move.

**What it is.** A brake disc against its cooling duct. `thermostruct2d.ThermoStruct2D` — the build-repo donor, imported verbatim, with cast iron passed as its own `SolidMaterial` — conducts through a $2.5\,\mathrm{mm}$ wall by backward Euler over the whole cross-section, which is `EMBEDDED` in R10's exact sense and which `L2/R10` duly refuses. A low-Mach convecting duct marches explicitly past its face. One `THERM` seam, $32$ cells, and **$500{:}1$ in clock because a conduction time constant against a convective cell transit is where that ratio comes from**. The **single-rate** solve is the referent — the same code path with the exchange interval set to the duct's own clock — so the zero-lag control is exact. The duct is written for this study rather than donated, and is graded against the method of characteristics, the erf half-space solution, its own first-order refinement and the discrete energy balance its wall flux has to satisfy exactly; its turbulence closure carries three textbook constants and none fitted, and reproduces **Dittus–Boelter's $U^{0.8}$ as $U^{0.836}$** unprompted.

**W90 closes with a function.** [[master-error-bound]] §4.2: $\sigma(\Delta t_{\text{ex}}) \le s_\Gamma\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert + C_2\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert^2$ with $\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert \le \dot\lambda_{\max}\Delta t_{\text{ex}}$, at $s_\Gamma = 9.4966\times10^{-3}\,\mathrm K^{-1}$ and $C_2 = 1.4288\times10^{-5}\,\mathrm K^{-2}$ from **one probe** and $\dot\lambda$ from the run. **It holds at every graded interval and is loose by $\mathbf{1.43\times}$ to $\mathbf{2.18\times}$ over $400\times$ of exchange interval and $2000\times$ of clock ratio, with nothing fitted to the table it is checked against.** The order column reads $1.011$, $0.908$, $1.037$, $1.248$ — first order until the quadratic term bites — and ratio $1$, the single-rate control, carries a defect of **exactly zero** rather than a small one, which is the statement that the instrument measures the lag and not itself.

**And the finding is which variable it is a function of.** The interval sweep moves the interval and the ratio together, so on its own it cannot say. Pinning the interval at $5\times10^{-2}\,\mathrm s$ and **refining the fast agent's own march** moves the clock ratio $8\times$ and $\sigma$ by $\mathbf{1.00016\times}$. **The interval carries the staleness and the ratio carries R9's flux transient** — two rules that were never disagreeing about size, only about different quantities, and nothing in the vault said so. That is what makes the bound usable rather than merely true: a real conjugate seam runs at $10^4$–$10^5$ where no single-rate referent can be built, which is why W90 had no number for three tiers, and a bound written in the interval is checked here at ratio $5$ and applies there because the ratio is not in it. **Scoped**: this holds for a fast agent that sub-steps internally at its own stability limit, and a frozen checkpoint cannot refine its own march — R4's asymmetry restated as an experiment the substitution campaign owns.

**W17's $W>1$ is exercised, and R3 admits it here** because both agents declare `bc_time_varying`. A trace carried as a linear waveform built causally from the *previous* interval is worth $3.6\times$ to $56.6\times$ per interval and $1.84\times$ over a $200$-interval rollout, **non-monotonically** — the ratio between a first difference and a second is the trace's *curvature*, which is a property of where on the transient the interval sits. A waveform buys an order, not a factor.

**W128 closes as the definition it asked for, and the second measurement is on different physics.** [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] **§0.4** is a third numbered convention beside the three verdicts and the five-field hole, binding on L7, L8 and L9: **a reported quantity carries the temporal parameters that move it** — exchange interval (`sigma_lag`, W86), clock ratio (scope, W131) and rollout horizon (`horizon`, W128) — and the horizon case is promoted to a **refusal** rather than a decertification, because a $\sigma$ without its lag is a bound of unknown tightness while a sensitivity without its horizon can point the other way. Measured on the duct's inlet velocity, a genuine design knob entering only the fast agent: $\mathrm dJ/\mathrm dU$ runs $+0.062 \to +0.186$ (peak, $N=100$) $\to \mathbf{-2.402}$ at $N=1000$, **changing sign between $N=150$ and $N=200$**, on a truncation branch where three finite-difference steps spanning $4\times$ agree to $1.8\times10^{-3}$ and the objective is bit-reproducible. **The mechanism is a closed-form lumped law written before the run** whose long-horizon limit the measurement matches within $10\%$ and whose sign condition — *the disc released colder than the air cooling it* — is a declared property of the release state, so the sign change is not an accident of one objective. **$N_{\text{sign}} = 200$ (one thermal time constant); $N_{\text{valid}}(20\%) = 800$; $N_{\text{valid}}(10\%)$ is NOT DETERMINED over $1000$ macro-steps.** The sign settles after one time constant and the magnitude does not settle after five.

**Two CS-10 findings lifted out so Phase C does not rediscover them.** [[general-coupling-scheme]] gains **§4.2**: at a field↔lumped `MECH` seam a lumped agent's constitutive law is *one half of an interface equation, never an update* — the update form is the partitioned-FSI added-mass instability, under-relaxation cannot fix it because $(1-\omega)+\omega g' > 1$ for every $\omega > 0$ at a repelling fixed point with positive derivative, and what makes the interface form well posed is the aerodynamic damping the update never asks for. It is stated with the fixed-Newton-count discipline a differentiable tape needs, and with CS-11's own field↔field instance where the same reading has a **closed form**. And **W132** is filed to Tier 1 as a silent-correctness row: two different physical quantities can be declared under one `response_half` — CS-10's fluid side returned the viscous traction alone against a lumped side returning the whole traction, both correctly declared `EFFORT`, `L3/C9` passing, and the blocks $1.8\times10^{4}$ apart.

**Three rows opened, and the first two are declaration defects rather than numerical ones.** **W133**: `effective_resolution` is specified as a measured spectral cutoff and a conjugate-heat seam does not have one — the probed operator is the identity to $\kappa = 1.026$, so every mode carries the same information and a rank-$m$ truncation discards exactly the fraction it drops ($81\%$ at the $m=11$ a cutoff-derived declaration would give). **Nothing `thermal_seam` published is wrong** — $\beta$ moves $0.05\%$ between $m=11$ and $m=32$, because an operator with no small singular directions has none to lose — so the truncation costs the interface *space* and not the conditioning. **W134**: the shared `fourier_basis` returns a **zero column at Nyquist** on a midpoint grid, latent because every other case study declares $m \ll n$. **W135**: `sigma` has no provenance field for which side's response its interface power was evaluated through, and the two sides agree *only at a converged trace* — the state a lag defect is defined away from.

**And the tier's mechanism is about the norm, and it cost the largest single error in the run.** $s_\Gamma$ and $\sigma$ were both interface power, both correct, both on the same seam over the same interval — and read through opposite sides of a bond that balances only at convergence. The bound over-predicted by $5.7\times$ to $8.6\times$ and every diagnostic was healthy. **Two quantities that are equal at the state a bound is calibrated at are not equal at the state it is applied to.** W58 is this shape on `cut_defect_bound` and W66 on the `THERM` flow variable; this is the third, and the general form is that a bond has two sides and a number read off it has to say which. A second, cheaper one in the family Tier 23 and Tier 24 both fed: **the protection and the destruction were in the same file** — the driver writes its artifact after every stage, which is what makes a ten-minute sweep safe to start, and began each invocation from an empty dictionary, so a single-stage re-run destroyed what the other nine had written.

Changed: [[case-study-brake-thermal-atlas-0.1]] (new), [[master-error-bound]] (§4.2 new — the multirate branch of $\sigma$), [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] (§0.4 new and binding; §9.2's L7 rules gain the bound), [[general-coupling-scheme]] (§4.2 new — the field↔lumped interface-solve default), [[gap-worklist]] (Tier 25; W90 and W128 closed; W123, W86, W116, W106 and W104 reframed; W133–W135 opened; **W132** added to Tier 1), [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] (§12 new, §6's W90 row closed), `atlas/CASE-STUDY-GUIDE.md` (the `Differentiable` members, which named an `FD` that never existed), [[index]].

## [2026-09-04] note | CS-12 — a rule finds the premise it never checked, and an FSI seam no certificate can see

**Why.** [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4's **Phase C first row**, and the one the ladder says twice must be settled before it is built rather than during it. §10 and §12 both name **W114**: a quasi-static structural agent is `EMBEDDED` and has **no `split-step` escape** — no time derivative, nothing to sub-step — so `L2/R10` refused every graph containing one, and taking that refusal would have made the whole compile-side content of the row *"R10 refused, again."* The row's own question is narrower and sharper than the blocker: *does $\mathcal R$ close across a genuinely **new governing family**?* Nine of the twelve real case studies before this one couple incompressible Navier–Stokes to itself; the three that do not meet at **one** seam, or **one way**, or **co-located with no interface at all**.

**What it is.** A 2-D wing section. The flow over $\Omega_{\text{fluid}}$ is CS-10's column **unchanged** — the same $\mathrm dx$, the same $208\times144$ domain, the same six $80\times80$ exposed `reference.WindowNS` windows at halo $16$, the same `ProjectedAssembly` with one global spectral Leray projection per exchange, the same porous inclined plate at $20^\circ$ — so the *variable is the partner*. Where CS-10's partner was a two-line algebraic spring with one degree of freedom, this one is **quasi-static plane-stress elasticity** over $\Omega_{\text{solid}}$, `thermostruct2d.ThermoStruct2D.solve_mechanical` on a $32\times2$ Q1 cantilever clamped at the leading edge, with $32$. They meet at the wetted surface as a co-dimension-1 `MECH` seam carrying **traction against surface velocity**, two-way: the flow's traction deflects the wing, the deflection moves the flow's plate. The referent is the same code path at a one-window tiling with the same structural solve, so the zero-cut control is bitwise (asserted) and the split differs by the cut alone.

**W114 closes, by the check the row's own *done when* asks for.** `L2/R10`'s sentence names a premise — *"the graph decomposes the domain"* — and the rule read `elliptic_subsolve` and nothing else. An `EMBEDDED` agent is now refused only when the graph contains **another agent of the same `governing_family`**, the compile-time signature of *"a larger region of the same physics, of which this agent has been given a piece."* It is a **proxy** for *"is this agent's domain cut"* and it is **conservative** — two agents of one family on disjoint regions are still refused, a false refusal that inspection resolves where the other direction would be a silent admission. **Four graphs' verdicts move and no measurement in the vault moves with them**: `thermal_strain` `surface-mech`, `brake_thermal` `as-built`, `thermal_seam` `as-built` and this one, every one a multiphysics graph where nothing was cut. **`window_ns` `as-built` keeps its refusal** — four windows of one family tiling one domain is exactly the class R10 was derived on — and that is the row that says the narrowing is right. Checked by a control: declare the structural agent with the *fluid's* family and `L2/R10` fires again. **The FSI graph is the first classical graph in this vault containing an `EMBEDDED` agent that compiles with zero refusals.**

**The gate is met, and what order the lag defect has depends on where you read it.** Over $240$ macro-steps the referent falls to a minimum tip deflection of $-0.030871$ at step $29$ and recovers to $-0.027220$. Against a **fixed** scale — the settled deflection, because a pointwise relative error divides by a deflection that starts at zero — the seam's lag alone is worth $2.700\times10^{-2}$ at its peak and the whole six-window cut $1.139\times10^{-4}$, a factor of $\mathbf{237}$; CS-10 measured $97\times$ at a field-to-lumped seam and CS-11 the same shape at a multirate one, so this is the **third coupling kind and the third time the seam is where the error is and the domain cut is not**. The lag defect is **first order** in the lag at its peak ($\log_2$ ratios $1.245$, $1.035$) and on the settled half ($1.216$, $1.050$), and **third order at the last macro-step** ($2.965$, $2.383$), because the columns re-converge — **W123** with its sign reversed. The lagged columns cross the referent **six** times at one window and four at six, at steps $30, 70, 97, 156, 171, 207$ for lag 1, while the pure-cut column never crosses at all: Tier 23's rule applied as a gate, the crossing looked for and found.

**And the added-mass question has a different answer from the one it was asked with.** A quasi-static structure has **no mass**, so the textbook fluid/structure mass ratio is infinite and is not the variable; what is, is the aeroelastic stiffness ratio $\mu = \rho(S_e^{-1}K_{\text{aero}})$, which is $1$ exactly at divergence. **Loose Gauss–Seidel does not diverge anywhere the structural model admits** — eighteen of eighteen $(E^*, \text{lag})$ cells stable, including a lag of $32$ macro-steps — and the boundary it would have to cross, $\mu = 1$ at $E^* = 3861$, is one a linear small-strain law cannot reach, because at divergence the equilibrium deflection is unbounded by definition. **What diverges is the UPDATE form**, at $\mu = 0.077$, thirteen times inside that boundary: moving a massless structure the whole way to its own equilibrium in one macro-step implies a surface velocity of $2.43\,U_\infty$ and a load $\mathbf{120.9\times}$ the one it was balancing, against CS-10's $5.77\,U_\infty$ at a lumped seam. **[[general-coupling-scheme]] §4.2 was written as a rule about lumped partners and is a rule about partners with no mass**, and its repair — the residual in the port's own conjugate variables — is here a $32$-square SPD Newton system rather than a scalar division.

**$\mathcal R$ closes, and the residual left on the transient is measured to be the accounting.** The structure's own strain-energy rate against $\int_\Gamma f_n w_n$: $1.055\times10^{-1}$ over the whole march and $6.21\times10^{-6}$ over the settled half, against $\mathbf{1.0000}$ for a static accounting that drops the deformation term. What is left on the transient is exactly $\tfrac12\Delta\boldsymbol\delta^\top S_e\Delta\boldsymbol\delta$ — the backward-Euler-against-midpoint difference of the energy accounting, since at a converged interface solve the fluid's effort is the structure's reaction at the **end** of the exchange while the energy gain is taken at its **middle**. `FSIRollout.macro_step` accumulates it beside the work so it can be subtracted rather than argued about, and subtracting it takes the residual to $\mathbf{8.66\times10^{-3}}$ over the march and $\mathbf{5.85\times10^{-8}}$ over the settled half — $92\%$ and $99\%$ of what was there.

**F5's gradient is exact, cheap, and for the first time in three measurements does NOT change sign.** $\mathrm dJ/\mathrm d\ln E^*$ by reverse-mode adjoint through the composed stack agrees with a central finite difference to $\mathbf{1.34\times10^{-5}}$ at $N = 120$, on a truncation branch monotone over an order of step with the cancellation branch not reached. Over $N = 20$ to $480$ it runs $+3.6\times10^{-2} \to +7.48\times10^{-3}$ with **no crossing**, so $N_{\text{sign}} \le 20$ — the shortest horizon marched, and what is measured is the *absence* of a crossing rather than its location — $N_{\text{valid}}(10\%) = 80$ and $N_{\text{valid}}(5\%) = 160$. **[AI Inference]:** CS-10's $h_0$ and CS-11's $U$ both act on an agent with a **slow internal process** whose long-horizon behaviour reverses the short-horizon answer, and $E^*$ acts on a **quasi-static** structure with no internal timescale at all — the only clock in the loop is the fluid's. One instance against two, argued from the mechanism and not measured across knobs. **§0.4 still binds either way**: read at $N = 20$ this gradient is $4.8\times$ its converged value, so an optimiser stepping on it moves the right way and by nearly five times too much.

**Three rows opened, and the largest is about what the substitution campaign can see.** **W137**: at this seam the fluid's block is $\mathbf{1.44\times10^{-5}}$ of the assembled operator, so `SubstitutionCertificate` cannot see *any* replacement of the flow expert — and the flow expert is the one the campaign exists to replace. W97 closed the same *reading* at a field-to-lumped seam by finding an **effort-convention** defect and repairing it below one; here §4.1's co-normal buys $190\times$ and the exact momentum exchange $1.79\times10^{4}$, and $6.9\times10^{4}$ remains. It is **not** a truncation artefact: mode by mode on the declared $M = 17$ the structure is $\sim5300\times$ the fluid in *every* mode. **The one-sidedness is the structure being stiff, and every aero-structure seam in an F1 car has this shape.** **W136**: `L2/R10/halo` has R10's identical scope defect one rule along — it decertifies an agent whose seam is a *physical* boundary with no overlap for anything to outrun — left open because a decertification that over-fires is honest and noisy where a refusal that over-fires is terminal. **W138**: $\tilde\Lambda_M$ **adds** two blocks the interface residual **subtracts**, both correctly declared `EFFORT`, so `L4/E7/passivity` reads a defect of $2.166$ that is an orientation artefact; `Connection.orientation` exists for exactly this and nothing reads it.

**Two more measurements that only this seam could make.** `probe.support_reach` moves **all $32$** seam cells at every amplitude from $1$ to $10^{-4}$ against a declared reach of $1$ — the same reading **W93** got on a frozen neural operator and called *"a fact about the architecture and not about the physics"*, here with the **opposite cause**: a quasi-static elliptic operator's inverse is dense. And $n_0(\Gamma)$ gains a **fifth row**: CS-9's *free* solid body has $n_0 = 1$ because a uniform normal velocity is a rigid translation; this **clamped** one has $n_0 = 0$, measured, because the same trace bends it. **It is the boundary condition and not the physics.**

**And the tier's mechanism is a premise, twice.** `solve_interface` solved the seam's equation over the **exchange** interval while the lagged scheme held the answer for a whole **macro-step** — four of them — which is a built-in overshoot of exactly `EXCHANGES`. It diverged at macro-step $9$ with every diagnostic healthy, and it would have been reported as *the added-mass instability of loose Gauss–Seidel*, which is the exact question the row asks. `CASE-STUDY-GUIDE` mistake 5 arriving on the interface solve, and the **fourth** composition-layer defect on this project to wear an agent's label. A second, smaller instance is why $\mathcal R$ is quoted where it is: the interface power is applied `EXCHANGES` times per macro-step and was read once.

## [2026-09-04] note | CS-12 verified — two gates marched past their own horizons, two sub-claims that were not

**Nothing was allowed to assemble CS-12 until its own gates had been marched past the horizons they were read at.** Two of the three rollouts on [[case-study-wing-fsi-atlas-0.1]] were shorter than the gate they served: $\mathcal{R}$ was measured over $120$ macro-steps against the gate's $240$, with the ordering read off **maxima** and no crossing search; and the $(E^*, \text{lag})$ stability map over $80$, which is **two and a half exchanges** of the interface equation at its loosest cell. Both were re-run, and both gates hold.

**$\mathcal{R}$ closes and closes harder.** At $480$ macro-steps, with the two accountings compared **step by step** rather than on their maxima, $\lvert \mathrm{d}E/\mathrm{d}t - P \rvert \ge \lvert \mathrm{d}E/\mathrm{d}t \rvert$ at $\mathbf{0}$ of $480$ steps and the worst instantaneous ratio between them is $0.289$, at step $0$. There is no crossing to march past. The settled residual is $49\times$ **smaller** at the longer horizon than the published number: $\mathbf{1.263 \times 10^{-7}}$ over $[240,480)$ against $6.206\times10^{-6}$ over $[60,120)$.

**Loose coupling is stable, on evidence the short map could not give.** Re-run at $320$ macro-steps with a floor of **ten exchanges of each cell's own interface equation**: eighteen of eighteen undiverged, and the tip drift across the $E^* = 5\times10^{4}$ row is $\mathbf{0.37\%}$ against the $5.0\%$ quoted at $80$ steps. The discriminator a survival flag cannot supply is now collected — the amplitude of each cell's tip oscillation over successive quarters — and it says the growth that exists is **not the coupling's**: $2.672\times$ in the **tightly coupled** column, where the interface equation is solved every exchange and there is no lag to be unstable in, against $2.664\times$ at lag $1$ and $\mathbf{0.223\times}$ at lag $32$. It *falls* as the coupling loosens, and at $0.60\%$ of the tip it is still under the plate's own $1.503\%$ settled unsteadiness. It is the shedding coming back after the release damped it.

**W114 is a genuine handle and not a bypass**, verified in `atlas/compiler.py` rather than taken from the page: `_r10_elliptic` refuses an `EMBEDDED` agent only when another agent shares its `governing_family`, the FSI graph compiles with **zero** refusals, the control — the structure re-declared with the fluid's family — comes straight back at `L2/R10`, and `window_ns` `as-built` keeps its refusal.

**Two sub-claims do not survive, and they are the same defect twice.** §6's *"subtracting the half-step term removes $99\%$ of the settled residual"* was measured over a window a $4\times$ longer march shows was still transient by $49\times$; over a genuinely settled window the correction is worth $-7\%$, and pointwise it is larger than the raw residual at $160$ of $480$ steps. §7.2's *"drifts by $5.0\%$"* was measured while the release transient was still running. **Neither number was wrong about its own horizon and neither was reported with one** — because [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] §0.4, derived from W128 where a *gradient* changed sign, binds **sensitivities** and reaches neither a residual nor a drift. Opened as **W140**. Both errors were **conservative**: the residual and the drift both improve when marched, which is the safe end of the class and is not a property the next instance has to have.

**And one latent defect, found by needing the quantity.** `WingStructure.stress` re-solves at $E_{\text{ref}}$ under the traction that would produce $\boldsymbol\delta$ at $E_{\text{ref}}$, so it returns $(E_{\text{ref}}/E^*)$ times the stress at the state it was asked about — a factor of $2$ at CS-12's operating point. **Latent, not active**: no number on the page is a stress and the method is called nowhere in the package. Opened as **W139**.

**The discipline this adds.** A stability map's horizon is per cell and is counted in **exchanges of that cell's own interface equation**, never in macro-steps; and the free discriminator nobody was collecting is to run the *tightly coupled* column through the same statistic, because whatever it shares with the loose ones is not the coupling's.

## [2026-09-05] note | PoC 2 — CS-10 and CS-12 assembled, and a search that walked out of the model

**Both interfaces live at once, on the same plate.** [[poc2-frontwing-results]] composes [[case-study-ground-effect-atlas-0.1]]'s riding wing with [[case-study-wing-fsi-atlas-0.1]]'s bending one: eight agents, nine seams, a field-to-lumped `MECH` carrying the wing's *position* and a field-to-field `MECH` carrying its *shape*, solved as a single $33$-equation Newton system with an analytic Jacobian and one field evaluation for the whole system. **It is an assembly and the word is load-bearing** — `FrontWingRollout` *subclasses* `FSIRollout`, so `cut`, `blend`, `project` and `band` are inherited rather than copied, and the fluid column and the suspension are untouched.

**Each parent is a LIMIT of it and both are measured.** $k \to \infty$ reproduces CS-12's $32$ equations to $\mathbf{3.37\times10^{-10}}$; $S_e \to \infty$ reproduces CS-10's scalar Newton *including its analytic derivative* to $\mathbf{6.36\times10^{-8}}$; the stress map is the expert's own solve to $1.79\times10^{-13}$; the zero-cut control is bit-identical. **$\mathcal R$ closes across both seams at once** — $5.93\times10^{-2}$ over $480$ macro-steps against $\mathbf{1.0000}$ for an accounting without the motion terms, with the ordering checked step by step and $\mathbf 0$ of $480$ crossings — and **it failed first, at $1.81$**: the plate starts flat so the structure's energy at release is zero, and the spring's was silently taken *after* the first macro-step. **A two-receiver balance has two initial conditions and only one of them is conspicuous.** The half-step term belongs to both receivers too, and carrying both halves the settled residual where CS-12's single-receiver version did nothing on its own tail (**W140**).

**Every seam gets its own verdict, from the compiler rather than from a rule of thumb.** Fifteen lines grouping `Decision.subject` by seam turns *"the graph is refused"* into *"seven of nine seams are not the problem, and the two that are are not the problem for the same reason"* — `wet` carries `L2/R10/halo` (**W136**) and `mount` carries `L2/R10`, both under a shared `L2/InterfaceMotion` refusal. Opened as **W141**. **And the one thing a live indicator must not overstate is measured**: over the sixteen corners of the design box, **zero** produce a different per-seam verdict map, so the colour is a property of the *declaration* and not of the design point.

**The largest finding is a defect this graph exposed and does not own.** **W145**: `run` enforced both experts' declared envelopes at every macro-step and `objective` — the path a gradient is taken through and the one every design search calls — enforced **neither**. So the first constrained search walked the ride height down through `Suspension.validity`'s floor and reported as its answer a design settling at **$2.24$ cells of gap** against a declared floor of $4.5$ and the $4.8$ CS-10 measured down to; the earlier loose-ceiling run's optimum settles **below the rolling road**. Marching that same design through `run` raises at macro-step $4$ of $120$. **The missing check was worth a fictitious $+6.9\%$** — and the pre-fix run's own last valid iterate, number $4$ of $30$, is within $0.7\%$ of the enforced answer, so the eleven iterations after it were spent optimising a model that had stopped applying. The mechanism is that **bounding $h_0$ does not bound $h$**: the settled height is $h_0 - L/k$ and the search buys ride height by softening the spring. `wing_fsi` and `ground_effect` have the identical asymmetry and the demo was a third path; all four now call one check that reads **detached** values, so CS-10's and CS-12's suites are $82$ green and bitwise unchanged.

> **A framework that can decline and does not decline on the path a search uses is worse than one that cannot decline at all**, because the search is precisely the thing that goes looking for the edge. The driver's `_evaluate` had carried the catch-and-classify machinery for exactly this since it was written; it had never had anything to catch.

**And how it was found is the discipline this adds.** The ablation was extended to run at the *optimum* for no reason except to replace an **[AI Inference]** with a measurement. It crashed. **No gate, no residual, no crossing search and no certificate had seen it, because every one of them runs at the reference design.** The same measurement then *falsified* the inference it was added to remove: the **suspension** seam goes from $0.49\%$ of the downforce at the reference design to $\mathbf{8.16\%}$ at the optimum — a factor of $17$, the opposite direction, and on the other seam — while the elastic-seam column **declines** there, because a rigid wing at that ride height makes more downforce and drives itself through the floor. **At that operating point the elastic seam is not an accuracy term; it is what keeps the design inside the envelope.**

**With the check live, neither named ceiling binds.** The search reaches $+19.3\%$ downforce, sits $2.3\%$ under the stress ceiling and at $52\%$ of the deflection ceiling, and is stopped by the **validity envelope** — $8$ of $30$ gradient iterations and $66$ of $200$ population samples declined, the trajectory limit-cycling against a wall the objective cannot see. The exterior penalty never fires, which is why a weight sweep over a factor of $16$ returns the same number three times. A separate column with the stress ceiling placed *below* what the envelope permits rides it to $0.37\%$, and that is where the constrained search is actually demonstrated.

**Against CMA-ES it is a tie on quality and the win is budget — smaller than this project has been quoting.** $1.0029\times$ on $J$; $21.2\times$ in rollouts; $\mathbf{4.31\times}$ in wall-clock. **W143**: every gradient-vs-population ratio on this project is in *rollouts*, and one Adam iterate costs a forward march **and** a reverse sweep where a population evaluation costs the march alone. PoC 1 measures that premium on its own page and never divides by it. Across PoC 2's three columns the rollout ratio is $18$–$22\times$ and the wall-clock ratio is $\mathbf{1.8}$–$\mathbf{4.3\times}$. A second, smaller error ran the other way and this PoC made it first: dividing by the population's *whole* budget rather than by the evaluation it peaked at, which flatters by $54$–$59\%$ in two of the three runs.

**Two more measurements were corrected rather than published.** **W144**: fitting log-log slopes, the ride-height knobs' central differences converge at order $\approx1$ where a central difference is order two — the plate's kernels are stamped on an **integer** box whose corner is a step function of $h$, so $J$ is piecewise smooth in exactly the two knobs that set the release height and the adjoint is blind to the jump by construction. CS-10 §6's own published table fits at $0.99, 0.63, 0.86, 1.01, 1.57$ and calls itself *"a clean truncation branch"*; its **agreement** claim stands, its characterisation does not, and the correction is now on that page. And **`stage_cost` was measuring a ratio between two differently-defined quantities**: a taped, back-propagated objective against a *bare* march, unwarmed, over $8$ macro-steps. It read a $4.8\times$ thread slowdown and a $9.47$ adjoint premium; warmed, matched and repeated three times it reads $\mathbf{1.9}$–$\mathbf{2.0\times}$ and $\mathbf{5.4}$–$\mathbf{5.8}$ — the latter *inside* CS-10's $4.3$–$6.7$. **The $9.47$ had already been explained on the page as the assembly's adjoint carrying two seams rather than one, which is how a wrong number acquires a mechanism and starts to look measured.**

**The ablation the assembly exists for.** Solving the two seams jointly rather than in turn is worth $8.4\times10^{-6}$ in the settled load — below the plate's own $0.562\%$ unsteadiness — and $\mathbf{4262\times}$ in the interface residual: the joint system is not buying accuracy here, it is buying a residual you can quote. At the reference design freezing the elastic seam is worth $4.1\%$ of the downforce and freezing the suspension seam $0.49\%$, an eightfold asymmetry that is the honest reason CS-12 could leave the ride height out and CS-10 could not leave the structure out. The frozen control is pinned at the height the **live** design settles to; the first version used the reference-load release height, which agrees to $4\times10^{-4}$ at the reference design and differs by $37\%$ at the optimum.

**No GPU, and that is measured.** More CPU cores make this workload $1.9$–$2.0\times$ *slower*, bitwise-checked first. A workload that will not use eight CPU cores will not use an A100; the vast.ai credit was not spent, and the one column that *would* be GPU-shaped is a frozen neural expert in the windows (**W137**).

## [2026-09-05] note | PoC 2's demo, packaged — and a compiler that refuses a real neural operator on screen

**The question:** what in this framework is actually novel, what does it offer the F1 north star, what are the drawbacks — and what can go in a GUI that is simultaneously new to a specialist and legible to an educated non-expert? Then: build that GUI, fully, and package it so it clones and runs on any machine.

**The answer to the first half is [[poc2-demo-and-novelty]] §1**, and its structure is the argument: four of the five points are **not novel and say so with citations** — the problem is not that we cannot simulate a car, the trained-model fix has two known holes, effort–flow ports are Paynter 1959, and power-bond energy residuals are standard co-simulation. **Only the fifth is the claim: a compiler that reads what each component declares about itself and returns a per-seam verdict — certified, runs-but-uncertified with the unmeasured constant named, or refused with the rule named.** Existing coupling tools couple; they do not judge. One sentence for why it matters: *a fast simulator that is confidently wrong is worse than a slow one that is right, because an optimiser will find and exploit precisely the places where it is confidently wrong.*

**Five beats, each a measurement this project already made, staged so it can be watched rather than read.** ① Hand the compiler three *descriptions* of the fluid expert — the classical incumbent, Poseidon-T as its own wrapper declares it, and Poseidon-T with the elliptic part declared — and the same graph goes **2 red → 2 red → 9 red**, with nothing about any model changing. That is **W93** on screen, and it **needs no weights**, which is why the bundle is 4.3 MB and touches no network. ② *Put it on the floor*: one click reaches a design below `Suspension.validity`'s declared floor, and the enforce switch turns **W145** into a side-by-side — the same design either stops at macro-step 0 naming the expert, or marches to $h = 0.0067$ against a floor of $0.0703$ and reports the largest downforce on the screen, within a percent of the recorded pre-W145 optimum. ③ Freeze a seam and price it. ④ Race the gradient against CMA-ES on one held clock, quoting wall-clock first — **W143** applied rather than described. ⑤ $\mathcal{R}(t)$ live, in three accountings.

**The packaging is `scripts/w146_build_frontwing_bundle.py`**: 4.3 MB, one command, macOS / Linux / Windows, Python 3.10–3.12, **nothing fetched at run time or install time past `pip`**. Verified end to end — `run.py --check` inside a freshly built bundle loads both external solvers from `vendor/`, reads the recorded artifact, compiles all three substitution columns and marches; `pytest tests/` inside it passes **79**. Against the PoC 1a bundle's ~90 MB of CC-BY-NC-4.0 weights plus an install-time fetch from a repository with no licence file (**W120**), this one is first-party throughout — because beat ① hands over a *declaration*, not a checkpoint.

**Two rows opened and three things found by running.** **W147**: `check_envelopes` reported two physically different violations with one sentence naming both bounds, so a wing *above* its free height was told it was below a floor; split, with the condition unchanged and Tier 30's 52 tests untouched. **W148**: W145's fix landed as **three** implementations of one rule — the exact shape that produced W145 — so Tier 31 asserts the property rather than the name until they are unified. And: the race was **measuring contention** (4% of its budget in 25 s with the march running; 75 s total with it held), the race was **measuring cold starts** (an unwarmed three-step race reads a $25\times$ adjoint premium against a warmed $5.06$ — `stage_cost`'s $9.47$ a second time, on the same graph), and the ablation **could be handed the wrong operating point**, which it now measures for itself instead.

**The prose is graded.** `atlas/demo_frontwing/explain.py` holds every user-facing sentence in two voices, and `tests/test_tier31_frontwing_demo.py` (27 tests) re-derives every number in it from `out/w141/w141.json` and asserts the absence of the vocabulary this project has decided it has not earned. A demo's copy is the part most likely to drift and the part nothing else in this repository looks at.

## [2026-09-06] note | Tier 32 — virtual control: the observability is there and the objective cannot use it

**The question, and it came from an identity rather than a defect.** [[master-error-bound]] §4.1 bounds an overlapping scheme by $\sigma\le C_\mu\Pi\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert$ with $\Pi=\max_j\sum_i\chi_{ij}\mathbf 1[b_i(j)\le d_i]$. A globally receptive agent has $d_i=\infty$, so the indicator is identically one and **$\Pi=1$ exactly**, pinned by the partition-of-unity identity for every halo width and every ramp — against a lever that normally moves $\sigma$ by $1.9\times10^{4}$. **The bound is not violated; it is vacuous**, which is a certificate becoming unavailable rather than a defect appearing, and the two have different remedies. Exactly one factor is left free: $\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert$. Attacking it means **solving for the artificial-boundary datum rather than lagging it**, and whether that is possible is a question about **observability** — which `probe.support_reach`'s $128$-of-$128$ does not answer, because dense is not well-conditioned.

**Gate 0 first, because flux balance died of its equation and not its solver.** [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §2.3's condition failed the reference-trace check at every $\Delta t$ and overshot $41\times/75\times/150\times$. The proposed objective passes: $J(\text{reference})/J(\text{lagged}) = 0.9884,\ 0.9907,\ 0.9760$ at $\Delta t = 0.05,\ 0.01,\ 0.002$, monotone along the whole five-point line — **on the column `L2/R10` admits**. It **fails on the column R10 refuses** ($1.0752,\ 1.0199,\ 1.0015$), and the failure carries **R10's own signature rather than flux balance's**: the defect grows with $\Delta t$ where flux balance's grew as $\Delta t$ shrank. A defect that accumulates over sub-steps is a per-window elliptic solve; one that survives refinement is a steady-state condition. The literal gate — every row — does not hold, and the artifact keeps saying so.

**Gate 1, the number the tier exists for: Poseidon-T's control-to-jump map is full rank $32/32$ at $\kappa=4.48$** — better conditioned than `WindowNS` carrying its own elliptic solve ($19.4$), against $1.95$ for the R10-compliant one, and stable to $1.005\times$ across probe states separated by $10.6\%$ and $1.004\times$ across three decades of probe step. **Global receptivity cost it $\Pi$ and did not cost it observability**, which is the hypothesis the tier was built to falsify and did not. The sharpest single piece of evidence is incidental: on one seam at one instant the downstream block's **DtN** is nearly rank-deficient ($\kappa=4171$) while the **control map** is well conditioned ($\kappa=2.17$) — two orders apart, from the same solver calls, so the two conditionings are not one number.

**And the other half is negative and belongs in the same sentence.** Minimising $J$ moves $\sigma$ **up** by $6.6\times$ to $21\times$, surviving Tikhonov over ten decades of $\eta$, truncated SVD over six retained ranks and a step-length sweep, with $\sigma$ re-evaluated by *marching* at every point and the best selected on $\sigma$ rather than on $J$. Meanwhile the monolith's own datum, in the same control coordinates, reaches $0.685$ against an available floor of $0.678$ — $97.7\%$ of what a perfect datum on that seam could buy. **The channel carries the correction; this objective's minimiser is not it**, because the jump has sources the seam datum does not own and can *imitate*. Agreement and correctness are different objectives, and this is the second time this vault has paid to learn that a residual reaching zero is not a solution reaching the truth.

**Five controls, and the one designed to be able to fail did.** The periodic agent returns $T$ **bitwise zero** while its jump is $9.9\times10^{-2}$, so the zero is informative. The field march equals `WindowAgent.respond`'s flux **bitwise**. The predicted rank-one null space appears **only** in the embedded column, at the bottom of the spectrum, with $0.9974$ overlap on the constant mode and a $0.707/0.707$ split between the two sides — which is what a net-flux constraint should look like. $\sigma$ evaluated at the exact datum is $0$ **exactly**, an identity rather than a floor. And Poseidon-T's $\varepsilon$ sweep converges at $10^{-1}/10^{-2}/10^{-3}$ then **breaks $5\%$ upward at $10^{-4}$** — cancellation against its declared $10^{-6}$ reproducibility floor, arriving exactly where the declaration says it should.

**Two things found by running.** The probe-state horizon was **absent, not short**: the settled state is a fixed point ($200$ macro-steps move it $6.03\times10^{-4}$ and it saturates by step $40$), so the first three probe states were one state wearing three labels. Spinning up from uniform inflow gives $10.5\%$, $7.9\%$ and $0\%$ separation inside the declared envelope, and the last mark reproduces `s0_state.npz` to $0.0\times10^{0}$. And **three attempts at *does it help* measured the wrong thing before one measured the right one** — the last failure being the instructive one: with the composition layer's projection restored, every control read $1.0000$ because at this cadence R10b's mismatch inflates $\tau$ and the quantity a seam datum can move sat **four orders below the number being watched**. A ratio of $1.0000$ is not a null result; it is a signal below a floor.

## [2026-09-07] note | Tier 33 — the envelope was a placeholder, and swapping it makes the bound non-vacuous for a frozen operator

**The question came from noticing what $\Pi$ actually is.** [[master-error-bound]] §4.1 bounds the assembled error at cell $j$ by $\sum_i\chi_{ij}R_i(j)\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert$ with $R_i(j)=\lVert\partial\mathcal E_i(j)/\partial g_i\rVert$, and then substitutes a **0/1 indicator** for $R_i$. That is tight for a local explicit agent and catastrophically loose for influence that is dense but decaying — the only kind a learned operator has. $\Pi$ is not a quantity; it is an envelope standing in for a sensitivity nobody had measured.

**Measure it and the certificate comes back.** At one exchange per macro-step $\Pi = 1.0000$ for **every** agent in this vault — the indicator cannot tell a local solver from a frozen checkpoint. $\Pi_w$ ranks them $\mathbf{0.186}$ / $\mathbf{0.496}$ / $\mathbf{0.790}$, in the order the physics says, and **Poseidon-T's bound is non-vacuous for the first time** — by $1.27\times$, which is worth saying plainly is small.

**The instrument reduces exactly, which is the part that licenses adopting it.** The periodic agent's influence is bitwise zero over all $13{,}924$ far-field cells; the local explicit agent's is **bitwise zero at $b=20$**, its declared $\rho s = 2\times10$, to the cell — not approximately; and at halo 1, where the overlap is narrower than the domain of dependence, $\Pi_w/\Pi = 1.000000$. So no past verdict can move silently. Gate A then re-derives $C_\mu$ against $\Pi_w$ on the sixteen configurations it was originally fitted on — with $\sigma$ **re-measured** rather than transcribed, reproducing `out/l6/w49_sigma.json` to $0.00\times10^{0}$ — and the spread tightens from $5.18\times$ to $\mathbf{3.71\times}$ while $\sigma$ spans $4.28\times10^{4}$.

**Three regimes fall out of one instrument, and the third is a surprise.** Local: horizon $20$ exactly, far field bitwise zero. Embedded elliptic: unbounded, far-field effective rank $3.44$, $52.9\%$ of it in the gauge mode. Poseidon-T: unbounded, effective rank $\mathbf{26.09}$ of $32$, only $7.1\%$ in the gauge mode, with all $32$ modes within $10\times$ of the largest far-field response. **So the checkpoint and the elliptic solver are both global and global by different mechanisms** — which is why `elliptic_signature` could never decide W60, and why `EllipticSubsolve` names a *mechanism* while every rule that consumes it wants *reach* (**W154**).

**Two claims died, and both were mine to kill.** The **rank-one derivation** the tier was designed around — that an embedded elliptic solve's non-decaying part is the gauge mode alone — is false: effective rank $3.44$, and per mode the far-to-near ratio splits into an exponential family ($0.168\to0.0003$ over $k=1\dots8$) and an **algebraic** one ($\approx 0.385/k$), which the strip estimate does not predict. The practical consequence is measured: pinning one scalar buys $\mathbf{4.7\%}$ of $\Pi_w$, not a collapse. And **W150 closes `refused`**: exact-plus-defect gives $\arg\min\lVert\text{jump}\rVert^2 = g^\star - T^{+}(e_i-e_j)$, so an objective built on agreement between **biased** agents finds where their errors cancel rather than where the truth is — which predicts every regulariser failing the same way, and a richer control space making it **worse** (confirmed at $3.21\times$). Virtual control is structurally wrong for exactly the class Atlas is built around.

**And Tier 32's open confound is settled, the other way round.** Both of its headline pathologies came from the R10-**admissible** column — but that column's lag baseline was $14.8\times$ *looser* than the scheme it names, so virtual control had an order of magnitude more room than the real cadence leaves it and still lost. The embedded row reproduces the published as-built $\sigma$ to $0.5\%$ unprompted, which validates the instrument for free.

**The fifth DD family.** [[atlas-and-standard-dd-theory]] mapped Atlas onto four classical families that **all assume a local subdomain operator**. §6 now carries the one that does not: **nonlocal DD** (Du, Gunzburger, Lehoucq & Zhou; peridynamics), where a finite **interaction horizon** replaces the stencil throughout the estimates and boundary conditions become **volume constraints** — data on a collar, which is what Atlas's halo has been all along. With the adjacent off-diagonal-decay algebras (Jaffard; Gröchenig–Leinert), whose inverse-closedness is the theorem that would let a pseudo-local agent inherit a local one's estimates. Measured, the three agents land in three different decay classes and the checkpoint is in none of them. **The theory being generalised already exists, filed under nonlocal PDEs rather than under machine learning.**

## [2026-09-08] note | PoC2-demo re-cut — the win leads, and the refusal becomes the reason to trust it

**No physics changed, no number moved, and nothing was re-run.** This is a sequencing and framing change to `atlas/demo_frontwing/` alone: `compiler.py`, the scheme, the probe and every tier's measurements are untouched.

**The problem was that the demo opened on a negative result.** Beat 1 was the substitution refusal — a real 20 M-parameter pretrained operator, honestly declared, refused on 9 of 9 seams. That is [[poc2-demo-and-novelty]] §1's point 5, the only row in that table this project can claim, and it is the right first thing to show a specialist. But §0 says this demo is the first artifact here aimed at somebody who *has not* read the vault, and on that audience the first thing on screen was the machine saying *no*. It reads as a project without a result rather than a project with a check.

**The positive result was already in the demo, already measured, sequenced third and fourth.** A real coupled multiphysics design search running end to end with gradients on two live seams, finding $+19.3\%$ downforce inside both the stress and deflection ceilings and declined **eight times** by `Suspension.validity` on the way — so the win is not bought by walking through a constraint — and reaching CMA-ES's best answer $\mathbf{4.31\times}$ sooner in wall-clock on the same objective and budget, both columns warmed before either clock starts.

**So the order is now:** ① the race (the win, and the one beat whose value a non-specialist can read off the screen without being told what to look for), ② the envelope (the receipt: $19.3\%$ enforced against $27.5\%$ unenforced-and-invalid, answering *did it cheat?* immediately rather than three tabs later), ③ the ablation (where the gain came from, $0.49\% \to 8.16\%$), ④ the substitution (**the trust layer** — how you know when *not* to believe ①, shown correctly declining a case it should decline), ⑤ the balance. **The refusal is not softened, it is sequenced.** `NOT_CLAIMED` still carries the tension whole and now names it where a reader meets it: ①'s $4.31\times$ is one *search method* against another **on the same classical solvers**, not a claim about the solvers, and the orders of magnitude still arrive only with learned experts that ④ is the framework refusing.

**Tier 33 lands inside beat ④ rather than as a sixth beat**, because the measurement sits *under* those three verdicts and says why they are what they are; a separate tab would split one trust-layer story in two. Until now the rule behind the verdicts rested on a 0/1 indicator, and at one exchange per macro-step $\Pi = 1.0000$ for **every** agent in this vault — the plainest classical solver included, which is the certificate being *unavailable* rather than a fact about any of them. The panel now shows $\Pi_w$ measured: $\mathbf{0.186}$ for the local classical solver, whose influence is **bitwise zero** past $b = 20$ cells — its declared $\rho s = 2\times10$ — over all $13{,}924$ far cells; $\mathbf{0.496}$ for the same solver carrying its own elliptic part; $\mathbf{0.790}$ for Poseidon-T. **This changes no verdict and could not have**: Poseidon-T is refused before the measurement and after it, and at halo 1 the ratio $\Pi_w/\Pi$ is $1.000000$, so no past verdict can have moved silently. What it upgrades is the *reason* — the compiler is not pattern-matching "neural network, therefore no", it measured a graded property and got the ordering right. Read by `engine.load_horizon` from `out/w153/w153.json` on `load_recorded`'s rule: never re-run, never transcribed, and absent, the beat shows its three verdicts without the bars.

**The bar-to-column mapping is deliberately not one-to-one and the panel says so.** Both Poseidon rows light the same bar, because *the verdict is a property of the declaration and the reach is a property of the model* — the beat's own point seen from the other side. The embedded classical row lights none: it is the control separating *global* from *global because it is learned*, and labelling it as one of the three columns would claim a declaration the screen does not offer.

**And building the panel found the one way it could have misled, before anyone saw it.** The obvious figure to print beside each bar is the far-field **Frobenius** norm — and it ranks the three the *other way round*: $76.07$ for the embedded classical solver against $9.78$ for Poseidon-T, because it is a raw magnitude in each agent's own units while the bar measures *decay*. A bigger bar beside a smaller number reads as a broken panel. What is printed instead is `profile_max` normalised by each agent's own peak, taken as the **worst** over the three probed states, and sampled at **two** distances rather than one — because one would let the panel call both non-local rows flat and only one of them is: at $b = 60$ the embedded solve has halved again to $12\%$ while Poseidon-T sits at $61\%$, unmoved. So the screen reads *still falling, slowly* against *it has stopped falling off*, which is Tier 33's three-regimes result in the one sentence a non-specialist needs. `::test_the_figure_beside_each_bar_ranks_the_same_way_the_bar_does` asserts the ordering **and** that the Frobenius really does invert, so the test keeps meaning something if anyone swaps it back.

**And the re-cut turned one thing up, which is W148's shape again: the beat order was written down twice.** `explain.BEATS` decided it, and `static/index.html` carried a second hand-written `TABS` array with its own ordinals and its own words — plus two prose sentences naming the substitution beat as the first one, and stale ordinals in five docstrings and three `--help` strings. Every test read the tuple, so re-sequencing in Python would have left the nav — *the thing a viewer actually clicks* — showing the old order with a green suite. Fixed in place rather than opened: the nav, the panel DOM order and every panel heading are derived from the tuple at load time, and `::test_the_pages_own_tab_nav_produces_the_new_beat_order` executes the page's own `tabsOf` through node, so the assertion is made against the screen rather than against the source of truth it is supposed to be checking. **[AI Inference]:** the general form is that *a test which reads the same variable the code reads is not a test of the rendering*. The two stale sentences here were caught only by counting numeral glyphs, and this vault's demo tests are written against Python objects throughout, so the same gap plausibly exists elsewhere in this template. Not audited.

**Verified from cold**, not from the working tree: the bundle was rebuilt and its branch cloned into a fresh directory, where `run.py --check` loads both solvers, compiles all three substitution columns, prints the three reach figures and marches, and `pytest tests/` passes 94 (was 79). `run.sh` is `100755` in the index. The bundle is **4.9 MB**, up from 4.3, because `out/w153/w153.json` travels with it — carried **whole** rather than reduced to the six figures the screen quotes, since a six-number excerpt living in the demo *is* the hard-coded copy the demo's own rule bans. Full suite **925 to 940**, the whole increase in `tests/test_tier31_frontwing_demo.py` (**27 to 42**). `scripts/vault_scan.py`: 211 files, 0 problems.

## [2026-09-08] note | What PoC 2 actually shows — one narrow claim survives, and two measurements cut at it

**Asked directly: what novelty does PoC 2 really show?** [[poc2-novelty-audit]] is the answer, filed as the adversarial reading of [[poc2-demo-and-novelty]] §1. It exists because the demo is the artifact most likely to be read by somebody who will not check, and the re-cut earlier today made it more persuasive without making it more true.

**One claim survives, and it is narrower than the demo implies.** The per-seam verdict is a **static property of the declarations**, and that was *measured* rather than asserted: zero of sixteen design-box corners change the map, and three descriptions of one unchanged model give three different maps. So admissibility is decided **before anything runs, per interface, from declared fields alone** — a different kind of object from a diagnostic read afterwards. The defensible statement is *"the hypotheses partitioned-coupling stability theory rests on can be written as declarable fields, and once they are, admissibility is decidable per-seam"*, and PoC 2 is the existence proof that this is mechanizable on a real two-interface assembly. Nothing larger.

**Everything else on the screen is competent and not novel, including the beats the re-cut promoted.** Adjoint-based coupled design optimisation is decades old and $4.31\times$ over CMA-ES on **four** parameters is the expected result rather than a finding; a search made to respect declared validity ranges is trust-region and applicability-domain practice, and **W145 was a bug in this repository** — a fixed bug is not a contribution; the ablation and the power residual are already recorded as not novel. **The re-cut sequenced these into an argument, and sequencing is presentation.** That is written down so the improved demo does not get read as an improved result.

**Two findings measured this session, and both cut at the central claim.** First: **the compiler has never certified anything.** Every case study in the package that builds with no arguments was compiled — `wind_farm` and `rocket` return `refuse`, `thermal_seam`, `brake_thermal`, `thermal_strain` and `front_wing` return `admit-uncertified`, and all six carry $L$, $C_\mu$ and a norm on `unmeasured`. Per **W56** the *only* graph that ever reached graph-level `admit` did so **because a module-default $C_\mu$ was being read as a measurement**, and the worklist records that finding it was the only way that bug could have been found. **So the positive branch of a three-verdict system has been exercised exactly once and that once was a false positive.** Two qualifications, neither of which rescues it: per-*decision* `admit` is routine and tested (tiers 14, 17, 24), and the graph-level failure is a **provenance** failure rather than a physical one — $L$, $\sigma$ and $C_\mu$ *are* measured (W1, W3, W49) and are simply not declared on these records.

**Second: no refusal has been validated against a real divergence, and at the seam PoC 2 exists to show, both decertifications are recorded false alarms.** Nobody has exhibited a seam the compiler refused that *would* have diverged, or one it admitted that *held*, so "it judges correctly" is currently a claim about internal consistency. And the `wet` seam — the fluid–structure interface that makes this an assembly rather than CS-10 again — carries exactly two decertifications, **W136** (`L2/R10/halo` over-fires: $\Gamma$ is a *physical* boundary, there is no halo to be inadequate) and **W138** (`L4/E7/passivity` is an **orientation** artefact; flipping one sign takes the defect to exactly $0$). Both `open`, both deliberately so, and the reasoning for leaving them is good — but **at the headline seam the compiler's output is its own two defects**, and good reasoning does not soften that for the novelty claim.

**Prior art the claim has not been checked against.** [[prior-art-and-novelty-atlas-0.1]] §0's table still has **no row for the compiler**, and that page admits the co-simulation tooling landscape was never surveyed. Two bodies are close enough that the claim should not leave the project first. **FMI capability flags** — an FMU declares `providesDirectionalDerivative`, `canGetAndSetFMUstate`, `maxOutputDerivativeOrder`, and a co-simulation master reads them to pick a master algorithm, declining schemes the FMU cannot support: that is *already* "components declare what they can do and the orchestrator decides what is valid", so the Atlas delta is real but narrower than *"existing tools couple; they do not judge"* — per-seam, a **named** stability rule, and the **specific** unmeasured constant. And **assume-guarantee contract theory** for cyber-physical systems (Benveniste et al., *Contracts for System Design*; Nuzzo & Sangiovanni-Vincentelli), an entire formal tradition of "declare assumptions and guarantees, check that composition preserves them" **with composition and refinement theorems already proved** — placing Atlas there would *strengthen* it by lending it those theorems while deflating the nobody-does-this framing. **Both marked `[AI Inference]`: recall, not a survey**, and exactly what §0's missing row should be built by checking.

**The economics are circular as of today, which bounds what PoC 2 can claim at all.** Composition is motivated by learned experts being four to six orders cheaper; the framework refuses learned experts; so the demonstrated artifact is a slower way to do what classical solvers already do. The demo says this. The consequence for *novelty* is that PoC 2's contribution cannot be "it makes search cheap" — only "it makes the trust question decidable", which returns to the two findings above, where it is shown decided *consistently* and not yet *correctly*.

**And Tier 33 is the most important thing that has happened to this claim, carried on screen as a supporting panel.** Moving $\Pi$ from a 0/1 indicator to a measured graded sensitivity changes what kind of object the compiler is — from a classifier over declarations into an instrument that measures a physical property and ranks by it. Two consequences the demo does not draw: it is **the template for the fix**, since the reason nothing is green is three constants on `unmeasured` and Tier 33 just demonstrated the technique for measuring that kind of constant *with a positive control*; and it moves the claim from *"refuses learned operators"* to *"measures how global an operator is and prices it"*, which is the better claim because it admits a quantitative answer to **which** learned expert could be admitted and **at which** seam — the question [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §2's climb-classically schedule actually has to answer.

**The highest-value next move, named in the page:** measure $L$, $\sigma$ and $C_\mu$ **on the front-wing graph** the way Tier 33 measured $\Pi$, declare them on the records, and the front wing becomes the first graph to reach `admit` **on merit** — converting the first finding from a hole into the result the package is missing, and exercising a branch W56 is direct evidence has bugs of its own.

Changed: [[poc2-novelty-audit]] (new), [[index]]. No code, no measurement re-run, no number moved: the two findings are a compile census over the existing case studies and a read of [[gap-worklist]].

## [2026-09-08] note | Tier 34 — the front wing's own constants exist, and it is still not green

**Opened by the previous note's own finding.** [[poc2-novelty-audit]] §3 recorded that the compiler had never certified anything and that the reason was **provenance rather than physics**: $L$, $\sigma$ and $C_\mu$ were measured in tier 0 on `window_ns`, at another state and another scheme, and were not declared on the front-wing records — so **W56**'s backstop forced `admit-uncertified` on every compile of it. A refusal that comes from a missing ledger says nothing about the assembly it refuses.

**All three are now measured on this graph.** `scripts/w157_frontwing_constants.py`, artifact `out/w157/w157.json`. $L = \mathbf{0.998719}\pm0.000001$ fixed-shape and $\mathbf{0.998735}\pm0.000002$ aeroelastic, both on the **contractive** branch so $T_{\text{pred}}$ is unbounded — fitted over 60 macro-steps of a paired march with rms log residual $0.000$, and honestly a *slow* rate: the error decays only $7\%$ over the horizon. $\sigma = \mathbf{4.535\times10^{-8}}$ and $\mathbf{3.173\times10^{-7}}$ at the declared ramp, measured as the same macro-step with the artificial ring held at $t^n$ against the same step with the ring ramped to the single-window referent's.

**$C_\mu$ on the record is W49's $1.2$ and deliberately not this graph's implied $0.56$.** Swept over six partitions of unity and two columns the implied constant runs $[0.00307, 0.5504]$ — a spread of $\mathbf{179\times}$ while $\sigma$ moves $965\times$. A constant that moves $179\times$ across the sample has not been bounded by it, so the sampled maximum is not adopted; $1.2$ comes from sixteen configurations elsewhere and holds on all twelve here with $2.2\times$ of margin. **Declaring the looser number is the conservative direction**, and the choice changes no verdict.

**What it bought:** `unmeasured` is **empty**, W56's backstop no longer fires, and four decertifications cleared — `E5`, `W49`, `W56`, `eps_tol`. **What it did not buy: `admit`.** Five named rules stand on the fixed-shape column — `L2/C2` (no measured `cut_defect_bound`), `L2/R10` on the suspension's declared family, `R10/halo` and `L4/E7/passivity` at both surface seams, and `R12` — and the aeroelastic column adds four `InterfaceMotion` refusals, which are a **named hole** rather than a stability rule. **Three of the five are W136 and W138, both recorded open *false alarms*.** So the audit's §4 point survives the measurement and is sharpened by it: at the seam this demo exists to show, most of what the compiler reports is still its own defects. The state is better and the claim is smaller — what blocks certification is now nameable.

**The positive control failed first, and caught a real defect.** The control is the single-window referent through the identical harness: with one window there are no artificial faces, so $\Pi$ must be exactly $0$ and $\sigma$ exactly $0$. `step_batch` reads a window's whole transmission ring and cannot tell a cut from the domain edge, so supplying the referent's entire ring also overwrote the **real** boundary. That reported $\sigma = 6.86\times10^{-5}$ on a single window where it must be zero — and *the same* $6.86\times10^{-5}$ to four significant figures on six windows, which is the signature of a harness measuring its own plumbing. Restricting the replacement to artificial faces took the control to exactly $0$ and the six-window $\sigma$ to $4.53\times10^{-8}$: **the first number was wrong by three orders of magnitude and would have been believed.**

**And the sweep opened W158, which is the more interesting result.** $\Pi$ moves only $0.750 \to 0.700$ across the ramp sweep — a factor of $1.07$ — while $\sigma$ moves $\mathbf{965\times}$. **On this graph the indicator does not carry $\sigma$'s variation; the partition of unity does.** That is W49's own *"overlap width is a precondition, the partition of unity is the mechanism"* arriving on a second graph and saying something stronger: the factorisation is doing far less work here than in tier 0, where $C_\mu$ spread $5.18\times$ (indicator) and $3.71\times$ ($\Pi_w$) while $\sigma$ spanned $4.28\times10^{4}$. **[AI Inference]:** this is [[interaction-horizon]]'s case for $\Pi_w$ presenting itself unprompted on a graph it was not fitted on, which is the strongest evidence for it so far.

**W159 opens as the cheapest remaining step toward a first honest `admit`**: `L2/C2` is the last decertification on this graph that is a missing *measurement* rather than a checker defect or a named hole, and the rule states the quantity to take. **W58 must be respected** — it has two inequivalent readings and `cut_defect_bound_form` has to say which.

**One user-facing claim moved, and it had to.** The demo said *"three constants the bounds rest on have never been measured, and while that is true every graph comes back runs-not-certified"*. That is now false for this graph, so `explain.NOT_CLAIMED`, both READMEs and [[poc2-demo-and-novelty]] §3 now say the smaller true thing: nothing is green, and the reason is five named rules rather than an empty ledger. `tests/test_tier30_front_wing.py::test_nothing_is_green_because_constants_are_unmeasured` asserted the old state and was rewritten to assert the new one — including that the two recorded false alarms are still present, so a change that quietly clears them fails.

## [2026-09-09] note | Tier 35 — three of the five fall, and the other two turn out to be the checker's own

**Tier 34 left five named rules standing on the fixed-shape front wing and said what each one was.** Three had a fix identified — two recorded *false alarms* and one missing measurement — and this tier took those three. **The graph now reports two objections instead of five, and investigating those two found that both are checker defects as well.** So of the five rules that stood yesterday, **four were defects in the checker and one was a number nobody had taken. Zero were facts about the assembly.** [[poc2-novelty-audit]] §4 said *"at the headline seam the compiler's output is its own two defects"*; measured through, it was its own four.

**W136 closes, and the fix is R10's own premise predicate shared rather than restated.** `L2/R10/halo` decertified STRUCT for being implicit with a nonzero stencil — every word true about STRUCT's domain of dependence and irrelevant here, because $\Gamma$ is a *physical* boundary of $\Omega_{\text{solid}}$, the structure is not tiled, and there is no overlap for anything to outrun. The requirement is now checked only against the agents `_decomposition_cuts` reports cut, and that function is R10's W114 predicate factored out, so two rules resting on one premise cannot drift about it. The exclusion travels in the decision's `uncut_agents` evidence **and** in its sentence, because a rule that quietly stops looking at an agent is the same failure in the other direction and it is the one that does not announce itself. **The control is the discriminating half**: give the structure the fluid's `governing_family` — the declaration `front_wing.build(struct_family=...)` already runs as R10's own control — and the halo rule decertifies it again on the same sentence.

**W138 closes, and `orientation` turned out not to be the field that fixes it.** $\tilde\Lambda_M$ ADDS two blocks the interface residual SUBTRACTS: both sides of a fluid–solid seam correctly declare `EFFORT` — the fluid the load *on* the surface, the solid the reaction that *holds* it, both positive in one shared direction — so the well-posed condition is their difference. The worklist row named `Connection.orientation`, and reading it would have been wrong: it is auto-filled identically on every seam in the vault, so a fix that consults it flips *every* seam, and **measured, that breaks three of the seven fluid–fluid tiling seams**, whose blocks are genuinely own-outward and whose sum is the Steklov–Poincaré operator. `Connection.effort_normal` names **which agent's outward normal both sides report against**; empty is the old convention and reproduces every pre-existing assembly to the bit. **Checked against the scheme rather than asserted**: `front_wing.solve_seams`' analytic Jacobian $\mathrm dt\,S_e + \operatorname{diag}(g)$ — the oriented *difference*, reproduced by a central difference to $1.15\times10^{-16}$ relative — is positive definite at $\lambda_{\min} = +3.469$ where the **sum** is indefinite at $-3.488$. The probe was assembling a matrix no scheme differentiates. Both surface seams go to a defect of exactly $0$; the seven tiling seams do not move.

**W159 closes with three controls, and each could have failed.** `cut_defect_bound` measured on this graph: $\mathbf{5.622488\times10^{-5}}$ fixed-shape and $\mathbf{6.054872\times10^{-5}}$ aeroelastic, in the **chi-weighted** form — L2/C2's own, the one needing a monolith — declared with `cut_defect_bound_form` as W58 requires, as the max over **80 exchanges**, spread $1.003\times$ and $1.178\times$. The monolith exists because `solver_for` builds the same class at $80\times80$ and $208\times144$. The controls: the identity $A(\{E_iR_iu\}) - Eu = \sum_i R_i^{T}\chi_i D_i$ closes to $\mathbf{1.0\times10^{-10}}$ relative with *signed* $D_i$, which is what says the lift, the weights, the cut and the forcing are all right; the zero-cut column on `SINGLE_TILING`, marched the same 80 exchanges, gives **exactly $0$**; two identical runs differ by **exactly $0$**. **The bound is tight to $0.05\%$**, reproducing tier 0's $0.2\%$ on a graph it was not fitted on, while the max form is $\mathbf{229\times}$ looser. And **W58 arrives again**: the aggregated surrogate equals the max form to $1.6\times10^{-5}$ while the contaminated sets are **not** disjoint — $512$ of $7424$ cells in up to three subdomains — so the theorem's hypothesis fails and the equality holds anyway, *tighter* than the four-window tiling's $1.00007$. Still luck.

**And the two objections that remain are W160 and W161, both opened and neither repaired.** `L2/R10` reads `ground_effect.Suspension` — $k(h_0-h)=L$, `stencil_radius=0`, two lines and no field — as hiding a pressure solve, because it declares the flow's `governing_family`; that declaration is deliberate and correct, since an algebraic closure inside a continuum problem is not a different continuum problem and declaring otherwise fails E3 at the seam. `L6/R12` is the more serious one: `_enforcing_agents` returns every `EMBEDDED` agent **regardless of family**, so on this graph it returns `['STRUCT']` — a *plane-stress elasticity* solver whose embedded solve is a stiffness solve, which enforces no divergence-free constraint and **is not a subdomain of the partition of unity at all**, so R12's own commutator identity has no term for it. Every agent that *is* in the blend is `EXPOSED`, which is the arrangement R12's own ladder calls the measured-stable one. Tier 34 recorded R12 as *"a real scheme finding"*; on this graph it is not, and that correction is the row.

**Both are left open deliberately and the reasoning is on the record.** Closing them would be a fourth and fifth rule narrowed in one session, on the one graph being certified, and the pattern an audit exists to catch is exactly that. Neither would reach `admit` alone. **The first honest `admit` is two premise-narrowings away, both diagnosed, both with the discriminating field already declared — and it is the user's call, not a blocked task.**
